AI 資本支出侵蝕現金流，科技巨頭財報拖累科技股 
                                   2026 年 10 月 30 日 
一、 市場動態 
1. AI 資本支出與現金流疑慮再起，那斯達克領跌：美股 10 月 29 日主要指數收黑，S&P 
500 下跌 0.30%、Nasdaq 下跌 1.38%、道瓊下跌 0.25%、費半下跌 1.42%，費半 ETF
盤後跌幅進一步擴大至逾 3%。市場下跌主要因 Alphabet 財報營收與獲利低於預期、
搜尋廣告業務成長放緩，加上大幅增加資本支出導致單季自由現金流轉負，股價重挫
12.64%，再度引發市場對 AI 投資報酬的疑慮，賣壓亦擴及半導體及軟體類股。其他
盤後公布財報的科技公司表現亦出現雜音，Amazon 資本支出明顯高於預期，近 12
個月自由現金流轉為淨流出，Intel 則因 PC 需求疲弱下修財測，使科技股盤後賣壓進
一步擴大。不過，當日 S&P 500 約 81%成分股上漲，顯示市場賣壓主要集中於 AI 相
關大型科技股，而非整體市場同步走弱。 
2. Fed 按兵不動但兩票主張升息，12 月升息機率仍高：Fed 於 10 月 28 日以 10 比 2 維
持利率於 3.75%～4.00%，Cook 與 Hammack 主張升息 1 碼，期貨市場隱含 12 月
升息機率約 85%。9 月 PCE 年增 3.6%，10 年期美債殖利率在 5%附近震盪。 
3. 中東情勢緊張，油價維持百元以上高檔：布蘭特原油 10 月 28 日升至每桶 115 美元附
近，WTI 亦站上 102 美元。高油價增加通膨與 Fed 升息壓力，也推升 AI 資料中心電
力成本，持續壓抑科技股評價。

二、 大型科技企業財報重點： 
1. 雲端業務全面加速，AI 需求仍未降溫：本週公布財報的大型科技企業中，雲端業務成
長普遍加速。Alphabet 旗下 Google Cloud 營收 307.09 億美元、年增 103%，TPU
系統銷售成為重要動能；Microsoft Azure 年增 48%，下季財測進一步加速至 49～
50%；Amazon AWS 營收 459.8 億美元、年增 39.3%，為近五年最快增速。Intel 資
料中心與 AI 營收亦年增 78%，創 15 年來最強成長。各家均表示算力需求仍高於可用
供給，顯示 AI 基礎建設需求尚未出現降溫跡象。 
2. 資本支出持續攀升，自由現金流表現決定股價分歧：Microsoft 於 10 月 28 日盤後公
布 2027 會計年度第一季財報，營收 922.79 億美元、年增 18.8%，營業利益率達
49.8%，自由現金流亦維持正值，整體財報優於市場預期。相較之下，Alphabet 雲端
業務表現亮眼，但廣告業務成長未達預期，且單季資本支出達 513.55 億美元，導致
自由現金流轉為負 90.65 億美元，股價於 10 月 29 日重挫 12.64%；Amazon 單季資
本支出則達 773 億美元，近 12 個月自由現金流轉為淨流出 424 億美元，加上第四季
營收財測中值低於預期，盤後股價下跌逾 6%。資本支出指引方面，Alphabet 維持 2026
年 1,950 億至 2,050 億美元，Amazon 維持約 2,200 億美元，Microsoft 則因第三方

產能交付延遲，微幅下修至約 1,700 億美元，三家公司均預期 2027 年資本支出將進
一步增加。（詳見圖 1）。

3. PC 需求疲弱拖累 Intel 財測，Oracle 明確籌資路徑緩解融資疑慮：Intel 第三季營收
165 億美元、年增 21%，non-GAAP EPS 0.52 美元優於預期，但記憶體等零組件成
本上升壓抑 PC 需求，客戶端運算營收年減 5%，第四季營收財測 150～160 億美元低
於市場預期約 171 億美元，盤後股價重挫逾 20%。另一方面，Oracle 於 10 月 28 日
投資人日設定 FY2030 營收目標逾 2,250 億美元，並承諾 FY2030 前額外外部籌資上

限為 200 億美元（股、債各 100 億美元）、自由現金流於 FY2028 下半年轉正，股價
當日大漲約 11%，有助降低市場對 AI 基礎建設融資風險的擔憂（詳見圖 2）。

三、 市場看法：
1. 市場不再單純獎勵 AI 投資，開始要求可驗證的財務報酬：Microsoft 與 Alphabet 同

樣大舉投資 AI，股價反應卻明顯分歧，關鍵在於 AI 投資是否已轉化為獲利與現金流。
我們認為，市場評價標準已由「是否投資 AI」轉向「AI 投資能否帶來足夠的營收成長
與現金流」，具備穩定現金流與明確商業化能力的企業將相對受青睞，而自由現金流持
續為負、高度依賴外部融資的業者則可能面臨評價壓縮。

不另行通知。
2. 雲端未認列合約持續擴大，AI 需求尚未出現結構性反轉：Microsoft 商業 RPO 達 6,770
億美元、年增 73%，Oracle RPO 約 6,640 億美元，AWS 與 Google Cloud backlog
亦分別達 5,780 億及 5,610 億美元，四家合計約 2.48 兆美元（詳見圖 3）。這顯示目
前市場疑慮主要在於投資回收期與資金壓力，而非終端需求惡化；只要雲端業者持續
擴充算力，先進製程、伺服器與資料中心供應鏈中期仍具支撐。
3. 資本支出 2027 年仍將成長，台灣 AI 供應鏈持續受惠：四大雲端／平台業者 2026 年

資本支出合計約 7,200～7,400 億美元，且 2027 年仍將增加。元大投顧認為，Google
擴大 TPU 對外銷售及 Microsoft 持續部署 GPU 與自研 Maia 加速器，將帶動伺服器
ODM、散熱及機殼需求，受惠供應鏈包括廣達、奇鋐、雙鴻、健策、富世達及聯發科。
融資面則宜留意 AI 相關企業公司債利差及再融資條件變化。
4. Fed 鷹派立場限制評價空間，短線震盪可能延續：12 月升息預期升溫、通膨與油價維
持高檔，使 10 年期美債殖利率停留在 5%附近，壓抑高評價科技股評價擴張。只要
AI 需求未出現結構性反轉，維持短線審慎，對科技股中期偏正向看法。
圖 3：主要雲端業者未認列合約（Backlog／RPO）規模（單位：億美元）
公司 未認列合約規模 相關說明
Microsoft 6,770 商業 RPO 年增 73%，約 30%將於未來 12 個月認列
Oracle 約 6,640 RPO 年增約 2,090 億美元
Amazon（AWS） 5,780 backlog 年增率達三位數

Alphabet（Google 雲） 5,610 略高於 55%預計於未來 24 個月認列
合計 約 24,800 約 2.48 兆美元
註：Microsoft 與 Alphabet 為 2026 年 9 月季底數據；Oracle 為最新公布之 RPO。資料來源：各公司財報，元大銀行整理
四、市場展望：
1. 科技股短線波動較大，中期看好現金流穩健的 AI 受惠企業：本週財報顯示雲端營收加速
成長與未認列合約持續擴大，AI 需求仍強；但資本支出攀升、自由現金流轉弱，加上 Fed
可能於 12 月升息，短線科技股波動恐將延續。中期而言，AI 產業將由全面成長轉向基本
面分化，具技術優勢、財務結構穩健且 AI 投資已能轉化為獲利的企業，如雲端與 AI 軟體
平台領先者，以及先進製程、伺服器、散熱等關鍵供應鏈，風險報酬相對有利；對高度依
賴外部融資、自由現金流持續為負的企業則宜維持審慎，並留意零組件成本上升對 PC 等
消費性電子需求的排擠效應。
2. 美債避險功能仍在，中天期債券風險報酬相對均衡：10 月 29 日科技股下跌時，10 年期

美債殖利率由約 5.05%回落至約 4.99%，顯示美債仍具一定避險效果。惟 Fed 鷹派立場
及油價高檔使短天期公債易受升息預期影響，超長天期公債則面臨財政赤字與期限溢價壓
力，中天期公債風險報酬相對均衡。投資級債中期維持偏正向；高收益債則須留意 AI 相
關發行人自由現金流轉負及利差擴大風險。

3. 下週財報焦點：Apple、Palantir 與 AMD 接棒，檢驗 AI 需求延續性：下週（11/2～11/6）
大型科技財報持續登場。Apple 及 Palantir 將於 11 月 2 日（美東時間）盤後公布，市場
將關注 iPhone 新機需求、記憶體成本對毛利率的影響，以及 AI 軟體商業化進度；AMD
與 Arista Networks 將於 11 月 3 日盤後公布，AMD 將提供 AI 加速器（Instinct GPU）
與伺服器 CPU 需求的最新證據，Arista 則可觀察資料中心網路設備需求。在本週雲端業

者確認資本支出持續成長後，下週財報將進一步檢驗 AI 供應鏈訂單能否轉化為營收與獲
利（詳見圖 4）。

上週重要市場資訊
 隨著法國距離下屆總統大選僅剩六個月，極左翼與政府間的政治對立升溫，然而
法國政府急需推動預算案以控制財政赤字與龐大債務。在內政危機加劇與工會
預告 11 月大罷工的背景下，市場對法國債務可持續性的擔憂升溫，將帶動法德
公債利差維持高檔，進而推升歐元區主權風險溢酬並拖累歐元表現。
 上週路透最新調查顯示，在 100 位受訪經濟學家中，高達 94%預期聯準會將於
10 月會議維持利率在 3.75%-4.00% 不變，惟全員一致預估 12 月將重啟升息。
儘管美國受 AI 投資熱潮帶動、經濟成長強勁，且美伊戰事推升油價突破百元
大關、加徵關稅使 PCE 通膨至少在 2028 年前難以回到 2% 目標，官員仍傾
向審慎行事。不過逾四成專家警告，聯準會恐落後通膨形勢，後續面臨至少再升
息兩次甚至擴大升息幅度的上行風險，預期美元大幅回檔的空間小，逢低承接買
盤依然強勁。
 路透最新調查顯示，受日圓疲軟與中東戰事推升能源及進口成本、加劇通膨壓力

影響，近七成受訪經濟學家預計日本央行將於 12月再度升息至 1.50%，延續 6
月與 9 月以來的較快緊縮步伐；同時，逾半數專家預估終端利率將上看 2.00%
或更高。不過，高達近四分之三受訪者警告，若高市早苗內閣提名的鴿派委員再
次投下反對票，恐壓抑日圓表現。在歐美央行年底前亦可能升息的背景下，日本
央行維持較快的緊縮節奏，有助於防止美日、歐日利差進一步擴大，避免日圓在
年底遭遇新一輪劇烈拋售，為日圓提供實質的下檔保護。

 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Oct/27/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週台幣匯價高點為 32.360，低點在 32.704，以 32.508 作收，較前一周收盤價
貶值 1 角 7 分。 
上週台幣走貶，周初，外資延續前周大幅匯出的走勢，台幣跳空開低，跌破今
年 7 月的低點 32.528；隨著台股表現不佳，外資賣超台股並匯出，台幣續創低位，
最低來到 32.704周內低點；而後台股低檔反彈，外資由匯出轉為雙向進出，加以出
口商積極進場拋售美金，台幣由貶轉升至 32.500下方；臨近周末，市場擔憂颱風來
襲周五可能休市，增添連續假期變數，外資再度大幅賣超台股並匯出，令台幣又跌
回 32.600上方，終場接在央行調節之下收在 32.508。 
＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣走勢偏弱。近期全球科技股因 AI 類股估值偏高及融資風險疑慮
而持續修正，市場風險偏好降溫，股市面臨修正，外資連續賣超台股並匯出，台幣
走勢持續面臨壓力。另一方面，美伊戰事和談進展的消息反覆，令國際油價節節攀
升，突破每桶 110 美元上方，引發通膨升溫的擔憂，支撐利率維持在高檔的預期，
美元指數也在 101.5 上方高位震盪，將不利台幣的表現。盤勢上來看，外資隨著股
市回檔修正表現偏向賣超台股並匯出，台幣偏弱，不過本周適逢月底，預計匯價逢
高仍持續有出口商拋售美金的需求，將限制台幣走貶的幅度。綜合以上，預估本週
台幣走勢偏弱。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：32.300-32.800

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣走升。周初中國公布 9月工業生產物價指數 5.5%，表現優於預期的
5.4%(前值 5.2%)，且第三季 GDP 表現 4.5%，也優於預期的 4.4%(前值 4.3%)，基本
面穩健支撐人民幣升破 6.6800 整數關口；不過周間美元指數持續於 101.5 上方高位
震盪，加以美伊戰事未見緩解，國際油價持續飆升，突破每桶 110 美元上方，人民
幣於 6.6800下方高檔震盪；臨近周末，人行中間價格連續走升，升破 6.7200整數關
口，週五最高來到 6.7180，顯示中國人行允許人民幣溫和升值的走勢，終場境外人
民幣升破 6.6700整數關口，收在 6.6690，週線升值 160點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6704，
週線升值 166 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣偏升。就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價持續偏升，將持續帶
動人民幣走升，加以出口商實質拋匯需求將支撐人民幣表現；就國際市場來看，最
新利率期貨顯示，本周聯準會利率決議有 9成的機率維持利率在 3.75%~4.00%不變，
不過年底前仍有一碼的升息空間，加以地緣政治風險未見緩解，能源價格高檔震盪
推升通膨與利率預期，美元指數維持在 101.5 上方高檔震盪，將限制人民幣升值的
速度跟幅度；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH日線圖中，各天期均線向下發散，匯價
維持在五日均線之下交投，均線呈現空頭排列，顯示空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預
估本週人民幣偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6300-6.6900

美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Oct/27/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 USD/JPY 震盪下跌。週初，USD/JPY 大致在 158 的整數關卡上下波動；
週間，AI相關概念股遭拋售，加之中東局勢緊張推升避險需求，10年期美債殖利率
跌破 5%，避險資金亦流入日圓，帶動 USD/JPY 下行最低來至 156.67；臨近週末，
市場避險需求持續加之日本 9 月通膨數據高於日本央行 2%目標，USD/JPY 一度下
跌至 156.06，後逢低買盤進場跌幅有所收斂，終場收在 156.86，週線下跌 113 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY區間震盪，主要原因如下： 一、美國 10 月 S&P製造業 PMI
為 56.0高於 9 月的 55.9且為 53個月高點，服務業則為 57.9低於 9月的 58.8，綜合
PMI為 57.6亦低於 9 月的 58.4，顯示美國商業活動雖略有放緩但仍處於擴張，經濟
基本面依然穩健將持續支撐美元；此外，中東局勢惡化使能源供應風險上升，布蘭
特原油價格突破 116 美元，為六個月以來高點，也將有利 USD/JPY上行。二、日本
9 月全國整體消費者物價指數（CPI）年增 2.1%，且 9 月核心 CPI年增 2.1%，為九
個月以來首次超過日本央行 2%的目標，強化市場對日本央行短期升息的預期，預計
為日圓帶來支撐。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY匯價處於各天期均線之
下且接連跌破 158 及 157 關卡，後在 156 整數關卡暫時獲得支撐，顯示逢低仍有買
盤進場，將限縮下行幅度。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較
高。

美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
         Oct/27/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 EUR/USD震盪下跌。週初，EUR/USD在 1.1200整數關卡窄幅波動；週間
受到油價及美債殖利率回落，外加法國社會局勢略見緩解影響，帶動匯價反彈至
1.1260遇到賣壓後回落，後續大致在 1.1190至 1.1250 間波動；臨近週末，歐元區 10
月份 PMI表現低迷，且市場對於法國政局和財政赤字擔憂持續，拖累 EUR/USD轉
若，匯價一度下行來至 1.1183，終場收在 1.1202，週線下跌 37點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD震盪偏空，主要原因如下：一、上週公佈歐元區 10月 PMI
顯示德、法表現分化，法國製造業及服務業均表現疲弱低於 50榮枯線，拖累歐元區
10 月綜合 PMI 由 9 月 53.1 下滑至 51.9，為四個月以來新低，經濟基本面承壓將不
利 EUR/USD上行；此外，法國社會及政經局勢不穩導致法債殖利率突破 5%，市場
對其財政及政治危機擔憂持續，也將不利歐元表現。二、美國上週初請失業金人數
降至 19.4萬人，為三個月以來新低，顯示勞動力市場依然穩健，且 10月 S&P 製造
業 PMI為 56.0續創 53個月新高，服務業及綜合 PMI維持穩健，顯示美國經濟仍具
韌性，預期將限縮 EUR/USD上行空間。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD
各天期均線維持空頭排列，且匯價持續承壓於月線之下，顯示短線空方趨勢仍未改
變，在能有效突破上方月線前仍以震盪偏空看待。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週
呈現震盪偏空的可能性較高。

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分，儘管市場預期強烈颱風「薔琵」來襲，週五金融市場可能
因颱風假而暫停交易，銀行間多預留帳上資金以應不時之需，但市況仍屬充
裕，即便有新增調度需求，也很容易滿足，令短率朝區間低位移動。 
週四隔夜拆款利率區間在 0.805%~0.825%， 30 天期短票主要成交在
1.65%~1.68%，利率低位較上週下跌約 1 個基本點。

二、 本週市場交投冷清，10 年期指標券 A15109 週四於等殖系統成交在 2.002%，
5 年期指標券 A15108則未成交，最近成交利率為 1.88%。 
週二 10 年期公債標售得標利率 2.001%，落於市場預期區間下緣，並創 2008
年以來新高，顯示市場持續反映經濟成長與通膨預期。 
考量通膨前景及國際債市走勢仍具不確定性，市場觀望氣氛濃厚；惟第四季公
債供給相對有限，可望抑制殖利率大幅彈升。預期短線台債殖利率維持區間整
理，5 年券及 10 年券交易區間分別估於 1.82%~1.84%及 1.98%~2.01%。

## Document 060: 2026年10月貿易統計

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年10月貿易統計.pdf
Report source: DIR
Date: 2026-11-18
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

日本

株式会社大和総研 丸の内オフィス 〒100-6756 東京都千代田区丸の内一丁目 9番 1号 グラントウキョウ ノースタワー 
このレポートは投資勧誘を意図して提供するものではありません。このレポートの掲載情報は信頼できると考えられる情報源から作成しておりますが、その正確性、完全
性を保証するものではありません。また、記載された意見や予測等は作成時点のものであり今後予告なく変更されることがあります。㈱大和総研と大和証券㈱は、㈱大和

2026年 11月 18日 全8頁

Indicators Update 
2026年 10月貿易統計 
自動車やAI関連の輸出が堅調も、原油高で貿易赤字は継続

経済調査部 エコノミスト ビリング 安奈

[要約] 
◼ 2026年 10月の貿易統計によると、輸出金額は前年比＋20.5％と 14カ月連続で増加し、
季節調整値でも前月比＋3.3％と 2カ月ぶりに増加した。輸入金額は前年比＋21.6％と
9カ月連続で増加した（季節調整値は前月比＋3.5％）。貿易収支は▲4,765億円、季節
調整値では▲7,672億円と、ともに 6カ月連続の赤字となった。 
◼ 中東情勢を受けた供給制約は続いているものの、代替調達の進展により 10月の原油及
び粗油（以下、原油）の輸入数量は前年同月とおおむね同水準となった。だが、輸入単
価は高止まりしており、中東情勢に起因する供給制約は原油高を通じて輸入金額を押
し上げ、貿易赤字が継続している。 
◼ 輸出数量（季節調整値）は前月比＋4.0％と 3カ月ぶりに増加した。乗用車や半導体等
製造装置、非鉄金属などの増加が全体を押し上げた。地域別では、米国向け、EU向け、
アジア向けのいずれも増加した。アジア向けのうち中国向けも増加した。 
◼ 先行きの輸出数量は、横ばい圏で推移するとみている。AI関連需要などが引き続き下
支えしよう。ただし、リスク要因はAI関連投資の先行きと中東情勢だ。AI関連投資の
持続性には不透明感があり、資本財輸出の下押し要因となり得る。中東からの原油輸
出には回復の動きが見込まれているものの、中東情勢をめぐる不透明感は依然として
残る。

### Page 2

1

2 / 8

【貿易金額】自動車やAI関連の輸出が堅調も、原油高で貿易赤字は継続 
2026年10月の貿易統計によると、輸出金額は前年比＋20.5％とコンセンサス（同＋20.4％、
Bloomberg調査）とほぼ一致し、14カ月連続で増加した（図表 1）。輸出増加の主因は輸出価格
（同＋13.9％）の上昇だ。円安効果があったほか、旺盛な AI・半導体需要を背景に半導体等電
子部品や半導体等製造装置などの価格が上昇していることが寄与したとみられる。輸出の実勢
を示す輸出数量は同＋5.8％と堅調に推移した。世界的な AI需要を背景に半導体関連財の輸出
が堅調だったほか、9月に自動車の挽回生産が進んだこともあり、10月の自動車輸出は比較的
強めの動きになった可能性がある。また、燃料高を背景に燃費性能に優れているハイブリッド
車への需要の高まりも輸出増を後押ししたと考えられる。輸出金額の季節調整値は前月比＋
3.3％と 2カ月ぶりに増加した（図表 2左）。輸出価格（以下、数量と価格の季節調整値は大和
総研による）は低下した一方、輸出数量が上昇し、輸出金額を押し上げた。 
輸入金額は前年比＋21.6％と 9カ月連続で増加した（図表 1）。輸入の実勢を示す輸入数量は
同▲0.2％だった一方、輸入価格が同＋21.9％と大幅に上昇し、金額を押し上げた。輸入価格
の上昇の背景には、中東情勢による原油及び粗油（以下、原油）や液化石油ガスの価格上昇が
ある。特に、代替調達先となっている米国からの原油輸入は数量面（同＋738.2％）と価格面
（同＋384.6％）で上昇傾向が続いており、米国からの輸入金額は同＋1122.8％と高い伸び率と
なった。輸入金額の季節調整値は前月比＋3.5％と増加した（図表2右）。  
10 月の原油の輸入数量は前年比▲0.7％とおおむね前年並みとなったが、輸入価格（同＋
61.2％）が大きく上昇したことで輸入金額（同＋60.5％）が押し上げられた。こうした中東情
勢に起因する供給制約による原油価格の高騰が引き続き輸入金額を押し上げ、貿易赤字が継続
する要因となっている（図表 3）。貿易収支は▲4,765億円、季節調整値では▲7,672億円と、
ともに6カ月連続の赤字となった（図表 4）。

【輸出数量】主要国・地域向けがそろって増加 
輸出数量（季節調整値）は前月比＋4.0％と3カ月ぶりに増加した。乗用車の増加が全体を押
し上げたほか、グローバルなAI関連需要を背景に半導体等製造装置や非鉄金属も増加に寄与し
た。振れの大きい船舶も下支えした。地域別では、米国向け（同＋7.9％）、EU 向け（同＋
15.7％）、アジア向け（同＋0.5％）のいずれも増加した。アジア向けのうち中国向け（同＋
5.4％）も増加した（図表 5左）。  
米国向けは 3カ月ぶりに増加した。前月に自動車の挽回生産が進んだことや、燃料高により
ハイブリッド車の販売が好調であることから、乗用車は増加した。医薬品は前月の反動で増加
したほか、半導体等製造装置は AI需要の恩恵を受け、4カ月連続で増加した。 
EU向けは4カ月ぶりに増加した。対米輸出と同様に、乗用車の増加が大きく寄与した。EU向
けの乗用車輸出は 3カ月連続で堅調であり、ハイブリッド車への需要の高まりが反映されてい
る。二輪自動車や非鉄金属、原動機なども増加した。 
アジア向けと、そのうち中国向けはともに 2カ月連続で増加した。アジア向けの増加の主因
は、2カ月連続で振れの大きい船舶の増加だった。半導体等製造装置や乗用車、非鉄金属など
も増加した。中国向けも半導体等製造装置や乗用車、非鉄金属などが増加した。

図表 5：地域別の輸出数量（季節調整値、3 カ月後方移動平均・単月）、海外景気と輸出数量

【見通し】輸出数量は横ばい圏で推移の見込み、AI関連需要と中東情勢がリスク要因 
先行きの輸出数量は、横ばい圏で推移するとみている。AI関連需要などが引き続き下支え要
因になろう。ただし、AI関連投資の先行きには不確実性もあり、その持続性には引き続き注意
が必要だ。また、中東からの原油輸出には回復の動きが見込まれているものの、中東情勢をめ
ぐる不透明感は依然として残る。 
11 月に入ってもホルムズ海峡周辺で船舶への攻撃が続いているが1、国際エネルギー機関
（IEA）の11月石油市場リポートによると、米国とイランの軍事衝突後初めて、原油市場は供給
超過に転じる見込みである。中東からの原油輸出が回復しつつあることは好材料であるものの、
同リポートでは下振れリスクが引き続き大きいとも指摘されている2。中東情勢の緊迫化に伴う
資源価格や海上輸送コスト（運賃・保険料）の高止まりが長期化すれば、物流制約に加え、海
外経済の下振れを通じて、輸出数量の下押し要因となり得る。 
米国向けは、AI関連需要を背景とした米国の設備投資の増加が追い風となり、増加すると見
込まれる。ただし、一部のAI関連企業では収益性や財務面への懸念が広がっているほか、米中
間選挙で民主党が上下両院を奪還することが確実となったことで、AIやデータセンターをめぐ
る規制強化への懸念も高まっている。設備投資の拡大が持続するか不透明だ3。設備投資の増勢
が鈍化すれば、日本からの資本財輸出にも下押し圧力がかかる可能性がある。 
アジア向けは横ばい圏で推移するだろう。このうち中国向けはやや軟調な推移が見込まれる。
中国の 2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDP成長率は前年比＋4.5％となり、2026年の政府成長率目標で
ある 4.5％～5.0％の下限と同水準となった4。中国では内需が引き続き低迷している。景気刺
激策による下支え効果が期待されるものの、需要の弱さが日本からの輸出の逆風になる構図は
続く見込みだ。 
欧州向けは横ばい圏での推移となろう。欧州では、ドイツを中心とした財政拡張を背景に、
防衛支出やインフラ投資が景気を下支えすると見込まれる5。これが欧州経済の回復につながれ
ば、日本からの財輸出も持ち直すことが期待される。ただし、足元では長期金利の上昇が加速
しており、これにより設備投資や公共投資の拡大が抑制される恐れがある。また、エネルギー
価格の高止まりによる個人消費の下振れも重しとなり得る。

1 「中東でタンカー攻撃続く、緊張緩和期待に逆風」（ロイター、2026年11月 10日公開、同 11月 11日更
新） 
2 International Energy Agency (IEA) “Oil Market Report - November 2026”（2026年11月13日） 
3 畑中宏仁・神田慶司「ハイパースケーラーの投資動向が世界経済に与える影響」（大和総研レポート、2026
年 11月 12日） 
4 齋藤尚登「中国経済見通し：不動産不況の底打ちに期待」（大和総研レポート、2026年10月22日） 
5 橋本政彦「7-9月期ユーロ圏GDP 減速しつつも上振れ」（大和総研レポート、2026年11月2日）

股價相對大盤走勢

漢唐(2404 TT) Buy

3Q26 獲利優於預期，客戶建廠需求暢旺，給 Buy 的投資建
議。 
投資建議： 3Q26 獲利優於預期，加上在手訂單金額約 1,939 億
元，有助於未來漢唐營運，給 Buy 的投資建議。 
漢唐 3Q26 獲利優於預期：漢唐 3Q26 營收 275.06 億元，
QoQ+5.47%；稅後純益 45.69 億元，QoQ+21.43%；稅 後 EPS 23.97
元。3Q26 稅後純益優於原先預估的 39.27 億元，主因毛利率優
於預期。 
漢唐目前在手訂單金額約 1,939 億元：漢唐 01~05/2026 年接單
合約金額約 674 億元，截至 05/2026 年已簽約未認列之金額(在
手訂單)約 1,939 億元。在手訂單約 24~36 個月可以認列大部分。
目前在手訂單金額的施工地區，有 2/3 在台灣，1/3 在海外。 
24~36 個月認列大部分的在手訂單可能是保守估計，2H26 在手
訂單金額可能還會再增加。 
漢唐對營運樂觀：漢唐表示目前建廠需求遠大於供給，因目前
人力短缺，尤其是在台灣，暫時沒辦法去接其他大型客戶的訂
單。由於 AI 與相關需求，現在客戶建廠的需求會延續到 2029
年，漢唐對整體產業的展望表示樂觀。 
群益預估漢唐 2026 年 /2027 年稅後 EPS 85.54 元 /117.99 元：群
益預估漢唐 2026 年 /2027 年營收為 1,070.59 億元/1,487.94 億
元，YoY+61.98%/+38.98%；稅後純益 163.04 億元/224.89 億元，
YoY+79.76%/+37.94%；稅後 EPS 85.54 元 /117.99 元。

### Page 2

個股報告 
                                        2026 年 11 月 13 日

2 群益投顧 
  ESG：  
永續環境是漢唐經營理念之綠色承諾，因應全球環保節能的需求，我們透過
工程設計、系統整合提升工程效率，減少工程過程中能耗。漢唐集成響應政
府綠色採購辦法，我們從原物料取得、工程施作、運輸配送、完工至客戶使
用階段，優先要求廠商提供低污染、可回收、再利用之環保產品，以達到廢
棄物減量之目標。 
漢唐集成根著台灣，從初創之施工安裝小公司成長為專業工程集成公司，有
賴於各地政通人和，因此，漢唐集成本著感恩的心回饋在地，不斷捐助弱勢
及支持培育英才，期望肩擔起企業之「社會責任」，讓台灣變得更美好。漢
唐股東會由全體股東組成，對公司重大事項進行決策，定期聽取董事會報
告，為公司最高決策機關；董事會為最高治理機關，董事會成員皆恪盡善良
管理人之注意義務，擘劃公司的經營政策與檢視財務績效，並確保公司營運
遵守各種法令；為完善公司治理運作、強化公司競爭力，董事會設置審計委
員會與薪資報酬委員會來健全董事會運作；董事會之下亦設有獨立之稽核
室，定期執行稽核業務並向審計委員會與董事會呈報稽核結果。 
公司簡介： 
漢唐集成成立於 1982 年，為一以「全面性整合服務」方式，專業提供高科
技產業建廠之系統集成服務公司；主要業務範圍包括高科技廠房各系統，包
括電腦、控制、無塵室、機電、電信系統之施作與整合服務。 
漢唐目前在手訂單金額約 1,939 億元： 
漢唐 01~05/2026 年接單合約金額約 674 億元，截至 05/2026 年已簽約未認
列之金額(在手訂單)約 1,939 億元。在手訂單約 24~36 個月可以認列大部
分。目前在手訂單金額的施工地區，有 2/3 在台灣，1/3 在海外。 
24~36 個月認列大部分的在手訂單可能是保守估計，2H26 在手訂單金額可
能還會再增加。 
漢唐未來還有先進封裝廠的訂單： 
大客戶於美國建廠的第三期工程的訂單目前漢唐已經有接到一部份，還沒
有認列到手訂單金額中，客戶的先進封裝廠的訂單還沒發包。第二期工程進
度希望在 2026 年認列約 85%，第三期工程的認列狀況要看客戶，未來還有
先進封裝廠的訂單。 
美國營收已超過台灣： 
漢唐沒有透露前兩大客戶的營收占比。2025 年營收占比_地區別：台灣 65%、
美國 25%、其他 9%。 1Q26 台灣 40%、美國 56%、其他 4%。美系記憶體廠
在台灣蓋無塵室，漢唐還是主要的供應商。 
漢唐對營運樂觀： 
漢唐表示目前建廠需求遠大於供給，因目前人力短缺，尤其是在台灣，暫時
沒辦法去接其他大型客戶的訂單。由於 AI 與相關需求，現在客戶建廠的需
求會延續到 2029 年，漢唐對整體產業的展望表示樂觀。

3 群益投顧 
其他補充： 
漢唐的費用比較固定，管理費用包含員工酬勞。漢唐追求與客戶長期合作，
如果是原物料上漲導致成本增加，客戶可以接受漲價。 
目前漢唐在全球的員工人數約 1,700 人，美國、台灣都會再增加人力，之後
也是要看下包廠商，看是否能擴大供應鏈。

6 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。  
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

Greater China Technology Semiconductors
Asia Pacific
Industry View Attractive
Our recent downgrades have generated investor pushback. We
acknowledge that demand remains strong, but believe exposure
should be reduced before cloud-capex deceleration becomes

visible.
Following Cloud Semis: Peak Growth, Less Upside  and Old Memory: Peak Pricing
Power?, we address the main investor questions below.
1. Are the cuts to Montage’s earnings forecasts justified?
We acknowledge that our revised 2027 RCD shipment assumptions may prove too
conservative. We would characterize the shipment and revenue revisions as
cautious, incorporating uncertainty around procurement timing, platform transitions
and supply-chain execution. These assumptions leave room for upside if customer

orders and deployment proceed more smoothly than we currently allow for.
Structural content growth remains supportive, and sustained order strength would
warrant revisiting the degree of conservatism in our estimates.
2. What justifies the multiple compression (across memory and cloud semis),
and where is the catalyst?
Our central consideration is to reduce exposure before the cloud-capex deceleration
becomes visible. Valuations can adjust as investors reassess the durability of

growth, even while earnings continue to rise. Peaking earnings-revision breadth and
weaker visibility into 2028 server production reduce our confidence in extrapolating
recent growth rates.
For old memory, the concern is also around the sustainability of pricing and returns
as shortages narrow. Limited fixed-price LTAs enabled rapid price increases during
the upcycle, but provide less protection when demand weakens. Our updated
supply-demand models point toward normalization into 2028, supporting lower

valuation multiples before peak profitability rolls over.
The potential catalysts are softer order momentum and slower price increases. Such
timing remains uncertain, but we believe waiting for these developments to become
fully visible risks missing the valuation adjustment. Faster deployment, renewed
earnings upgrades and sustained capex acceleration beyond 1Q27 would challenge
our cautious view. (Continued)
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
November 15, 2026 03:36 PM GMT

### Page 2

M
 Idea
2

3. Have we changed our server or CPU shipment forecasts? If demand remains strong, 
why act now?
We have made no such industry-wide revisions. The hyperscalers’ 3Q results confirmed 
strong current spending. However, following the US midterms, our US team shifted 
approximately 5GW of expected US datacenter capacity additions from 2028 into 2029, 
equivalent to around 20% of previously expected 2028 additions (see Buy the Drop: AI 
Investment Opportunities Post the US Midterms). Our US team's hyperscaler capex 
forecasts remained unchanged at US$1.6trn and US$1.8trn for 2027 and 2028. This 
creates greater uncertainty around the timing of equipment procurement and deployment 
even with spending intentions intact.
We believe the combination of the completed 3Q earnings season and greater post-
election execution uncertainty provides a reasonable point to incorporate more risk. 
Current demand can remain strong while the balance of potential earnings revisions and 
valuation outcomes becomes less attractive.
4. Why retain Overweight on CXMT and Nanya Tech?
We differentiate by product mix, technology migration and valuation. Niche memory has a 
smaller supply base, more volatile pricing and generally less direct exposure to AI 
demand. Its pricing can respond sharply to relatively modest changes in supply, customer 
inventories and consumer demand. These characteristics make recent scarcity-driven 
profitability more vulnerable as supply catches up.
CXMT and Nanya also face memory-cycle risk. However, CXMT has additional growth 
drivers from capacity expansion, localization and process tech migration. We also continue 
to see tight DDR5 supply, with CXMT management indicating that customers had reserved 
2027 capacity for relevant enterprise products. For Nanya, stronger process technology 
and a clearer DDR5 migration path differentiate it from other DDR4 suppliers in our 
coverage, with DDR5 already contributing approximately 10% of its revenue.
Aspeed and Montage have concentrated exposure to server deployment and the cloud 
investment cycle. Their content-growth opportunities remain intact, but their earnings 
trajectories and valuations are particularly sensitive to changes in deployment timing and 
expectations for cloud-spending growth. We therefore see a stronger case for reducing 
exposure ahead of visible deceleration.

陳玫芬 合格證券投資分析人員 & CFP 
Lisa.mf.chen@yuanta.com 
陳亮齊 
chrischenlc@yuanta.com

InP 基板缺貨影響光通訊，3Q26 營運低於預期且 4Q 估計下修 
3Q26 營收 3.2 億元，季增 25%且年增 27%；毛利率/營益率為 39%/18%，
季增 4.7 ppts/9.7 ppts，EPS 1.26 元，季增 214%/年增 68%，低於預期 23%；
光通訊占比為 25%，低於 1Q26/2Q26 的 45%/37%。光通訊比重低使毛利率
較原估計低 3.9 ppts。估計 4Q26 營收 3.77 億元，季增 18% (10 月營收已達
1.24 億元)，下修 14%，主因 InP 基板持續缺貨中；毛利率下修 4.4ppts 至
38.8%；EPS 估計為 1.31 元，下修 38%。 
GaAs 應用之研發案增加，包含 VCSEL、QD Laser 及 PIN 
IET-KY 的 MBE 磊晶包含 GaAs(砷化鎵)、InP(磷化銦)、GaSb(銻化鎵)等材
料，雖然 InP 現階段基板不足，惟 GaAs 的研發案近期有所斬獲，如(1) 射頻
用於國防；(2) VCSEL，200G 射頻用於光通訊(市場尚未有量產者)；(3) 
QD(量子點)雷射，發射端已於 2026 年中送樣再調整結構中；(4) PIN，用於
光通訊的接收端。此外，GaSb(銻化鎵)估計 2026 年增 65%且占比 19%以國
防應用為主，近期亦有新的商業需求正與客戶洽談中。 
2026 及 2027 年 EPS 估計下修 31%及 29%，目前評價合理 
IET-KY 目前光通訊出貨以 100G 及 200G 的 PD 為主；公司無採用上游中國
大陸設廠之 AXT 的 InP 基板，目前採日系基板為主且今年處於即到貨即用的
緊俏狀態。德系供應商期待後續產能提升但仍需時間，本中心估計 2026 年
光通訊占比由 2025 年的 44%降至 35%，2027 年回到占比 44%。本中心下
修估計 2026/2027 年營收 13%/13%至 12.85 億/18.61 億元，年增 19%/45%，
毛利率 40%/43%，下修 3.5/4.7 百分點，EPS 為 4.63 元/9.03 元，年增
184%/95%，下修 31%/29%。因 InP 基板取得不確定性而下修目標本益比，
採估計 2027 年 EPS 以 45 倍目標本益比(前次採 57 倍)給予目標價 410 元，
目前評價合理給予持有之投資評等。 
元大觀點 
⧫  
InP 基板缺貨影響使 3Q26 獲利低於預期 23%至 EPS 1.26 元，且光通
訊占比為 25%，低於 1Q26/2Q26 的 45%/37%；4Q 估 EPS 1.31 元。 
⧫  
GaAs 應用之研發案增加，包含 VCSEL、QD Laser 及 PIN，有機會於
2028 年貢獻；此外，GaSb 於國防及商業需求均提升。 
⧫  
採日系基板為主且處於即到貨即用的緊俏狀態；2026 及 2027 年 EPS
估計下修 31%及 29%至 4.6 及 9.0 元，呈高度成長惟目前評價合理。

### Page 3

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：半導體 2026 年 11 月 17 日 IET 第3 頁，共 14頁
上游 InP 基板緊缺難以完全解決，惟公司本身可望於 2H27 稍改善
IET-KY 目前光通訊出貨以 100G 及 200G 的 PD 為主，InP 基板短缺影響營運；因公司若干產品用
於美國軍工國防，故無採用上游中國大陸設廠之 AXT 的 InP 基板，目前採日系基板比重達 80%，
而且今年處於即到貨即用的緊俏狀態。德系供應商期待後續產能提升，且部份客戶自行提供基板；
然而整體基板於市場供需仍然緊俏，其他磊晶廠商亦積極搶占基板料源，基板供應商均已規劃大幅
度擴充產能惟仍需時間，估計二年內均呈緊缺狀況，公司則於 2H27 稍微改善。
GaAs(砷化鎵)應用之研發案增加，GaSb(銻化鎵)亦有新的商業需求
IET-KY 的 MBE 磊晶包含 GaAs(砷化鎵)、InP(磷化銦)、GaSb(銻化鎵)等材料，雖然 InP 現階段基
板不足，惟 GaAs 的研發案近期有所斬獲，如(1)射頻用於國防；(2)VCSEL，200G 射頻用於光通訊

(市場尚未有量產者)；(3)QD(量子點)雷射，發射端已於 2026 年中送樣再調整結構中；(4)PIN，用
於光通訊的接收端。此外，GaSb(銻化鎵)估計 2026 年增 65%以國防應用為主，近期亦有新的商業
需求正與客戶洽談中。
光聖換股已完成，3Q26 於業外挹注股利收入 0.16 億元
IET-KY 與光聖換股已於 5 月完成，透過股份交換結盟後，雙方相互持股比例分別為光聖持有 IET-
KY 約 15.26% 普通股股權，IET-KY 持有光聖約 1.79% 普通股股權。3Q26 於業外挹注股利收入
0.16 億元，短期尚未有其他本業的助益，長期有機會與其子公司合作開發產品。
MBE 磊晶在厚度控制及品質具優勢，估 2026/2027 年營運明顯年增但較先前保守
IET-KY 營運主體位於美國德州，以 MBE(Molecular Beam Epitaxy；分子束磊晶)技術為核心，同時
具備機器設備開發能力。MBE 磊晶在厚度控制及品質上準確度高於 MOCVD，適合新品研發及高階

產品。高階光模組應用於 2026 年以 400G 及 800G 為主流，多採用 100G 的 PD。現階段 IET 出貨
以 100G PD 及 200G PD(可用於 200G 光模組)為主。公司 MBE 機台為 16 台，估 2027 年增至 19
台。由於 InP 基板缺貨難解，本中心下修 2026/2027 年營收 13%/13%至 12.85 億/18.61 億元，年
增 19%/45%，毛利率 40%/43%，下修 3.5/4.7 百分點，EPS 為 4.63 元/9.03 元，年增 184%/95%，
下修 31%/29%。

資料來源：公司資料、元大投顧整理

圖 5：2025、2026F 及 2027F 年估計營收及營益率逐年走高

資料來源：公司資料、元大投顧預估 
MBE 透過分子束提高均勻性、純度等，高階光通訊趨勢有利營運增長 
MBE (Molecular Beam Epitaxy)分子束磊晶製程為：(1)分子束形成：將坩鍋中的原料透過噴射源精
確加熱以昇華、蒸發分子形成分子束，分子如砷、鎵、磷、銦，噴嘴以石英克努森容器（Kundsen 
Cell）為主；(2)表面附著與擴散：分子束抵達加熱過的基板表面並吸附後將在晶格能量最低的地方
進行鍵結；(3)外延生長：透過精確控制基板溫度與分子束通量，薄膜會依照基板的晶體結構進行層
層堆疊最終形成磊晶。 
噴射源重要性在於透過熱管理、快門（Shutter）控制提供穩定、均勻的分子束，使薄膜品質穩定。
MBE 另外一大重要的設備為反射高能電子繞射儀 (Refection High-Energy Electron Diffraction, 
RHEED)，透過 RHEED 可以進行生長速率控制、生長狀態監控以即時控管磊晶品質。 
MBE 相較於 MOCVD 的優勢於：(1)精準度：透過分子束的控制更精準的製造且控制厚度；(2)減少
雜質：在原料端由於減去了前驅物的使用使材料純度更高，且基板遷移摻雜問題較小。然而，生長
速度較慢與且耗材要求較高，整體成本較高。

### Page 5

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：半導體 2026 年 11 月 17 日 IET 第5 頁，共 14頁  
產業概況  
光收發模組明顯成長且 800G 及 1.6T 零件更高階；NPO/CPO 數量低但高成長 
AI 對高速網路傳輸速率的需求持續攀升，光收發模組 (Optical Transceiver) 扮演關鍵角色；光收發
模組於 400G 以下且市場穩定；然而 400G 以上估年增 2026/2027 年為 80%/43%至 1 億/1.45 億個；
2026 年主流為 400G，2027 及 2028 年主流為 800G 且未來升級至 1.6T 以上則採更高階零組件，
如 Interposer、TEC 及 Submount 等電子級精密陶瓷。NPO/CPO 仍為初始階段，2026/2027/2028
年估 40 萬/320 萬/2,200 萬個：2027/2028 年增 680%/595%，惟數量遠低於光收發模組；CPO 將
OE 與交換機的 ASIC 共同封裝，降低傳輸功耗至 1/3；NPO 僅將 OE 與 ASIC 放置在交換機內同一
PCB 基板上，降低交換機傳輸功耗至 50%；二者均需插拔式 ELSFP 模組。 
圖 6：光收發模組(Optical Transceiver)市場預估數量成長；CPO/NPO 市場仍小但成長性高

插拔式光收發模組以光晶片、PCB 及 DSP 最短缺 
插拔式光收發模組以光晶片、PCB 及 DSP 最短缺；內部架構以 DSP 為主晶片，其與 TIA 及 Driver
組成 EIC；PIC 則包含 PD、 LD、調變器； OE(光引擎)包含 EIC 與 PIC，所有零件在傳輸速率提升
下持續升級。PCB 在高階光模組需 mSAP 製程具門檻， DSP 由國際少數廠商寡占使供需相當緊俏，
內部的發射及接收端光元件晶粒以國際大廠為主。 
圖 7：光收發模組零件供應商分工架構(左)；光晶片上下游供應商以大廠為主(右)

資料來源：網路資料、元大投顧整理

圖 9：光收發模組中的 OE (左)；CPO/NPO 將 OE 置交換機內與 ASIC 共同封裝/同一 PCB 板 (右)

資料來源：市場資料、元大投顧整理

資料傳輸更高速使光晶片數量及規格提升；CPO/NPO 亦需插拔式光模組 
AI 交換機(Switch) 單埠（Port）的傳輸資料量主要取決於交換機的世代技術，2026 年主流為單 Port 
800G 的傳輸速率採 8 通道且單埠每秒可傳輸約 100 GB 的資料；1.6T（1.6 Tbps）採 8 通道且單
埠每秒可傳輸達 200 GB 的資料；未來若發展 3.2T 將採 8 通道且單埠每秒可傳輸達 400 GB 的資
料。 
此外，CPO（Co-Packaged Optics；共同封裝光學）光子積體電路（PIC）與電子積體電路（EIC）
共同封裝在同一個載板上，目前市場的 CPO/NPO 之 AI 交換機(Switch)，採 ELSFP（External 
Laser Small Form Pluggable，外接雷射光源模組），調變晶片靠近 ASIC 位置以縮短傳輸距離及降
低功耗；ELSFP 將容易受温度影響且壽命較短的雷射光源獨立出來，放在主機板邊緣作為標準化
插拔模組，不僅提升散熱效率，也解決 CPO/NPO 系統的維護難題。

### Page 7

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：半導體 2026 年 11 月 17 日 IET 第7 頁，共 14頁
獲利調整與股票評價
MBE 磊晶法於高階應用具前景故提升機台產能，惟目前評價合理
IET-KY 為美國製造的 MBE(Molecular Beam Epitaxy；分子束磊晶)製程 III-V 族化合物磊晶廠，光
通訊(光纖網路)產品以應用於光收發模組為主要動能，占營收比重 2024 年的 29%升至 2025 年的
44%，帶動 2025 年毛利率年增 20 百分點至 36%。IET 的 100G 及 200G PD 可應用於 800G 及 1.6T
的光收發模組；未來市場朝 3.2T 邁進時需要 400G PD 且公司已進入研發階段，惟 2029 年後才有
顯著成長動能。
公司 MBE 機台為 16 台，估 2027 年增至 19 台。由於 InP 基板缺貨難解，本中心估計 2026 年光通
訊占比由 2025 年的 44%降至 35%，金額年減 5%；2027 年回到占比 44%，金額年增 79%。本中

心下修估計 2026/2027 年營收 13%/13%至 12.85 億/18.61 億元，年增 19%/45%，毛利率 40%/43%，
下修 3.5/4.7 百分點，EPS 為 4.63 元/9.03 元，年增 184%/95%，下修 31%/29%。因 InP 基板取得
不確定性而下修目標本益比，採估計 2027 年 EPS 以 45 倍目標本益比(前次採 57 倍)給予目標價
410 元，目前評價合理給予持有之投資評等。

公司簡介  
生產基地位於美國的Ⅲ-Ⅴ族化合物半導體 MBE 磊晶製造商，光通訊比重最高且持續攀升 
IET-KY 成立於 2011 年，營運主體位於美國德州，為Ⅲ-Ⅴ族化合物半導體磊晶片代工廠，以 MBE 磊晶技術為核心，產品
包含 GaAs(砷化鎵)、InP(磷化銦)，GaSb(銻化鎵)；應用領域包含無線通訊、衛星通訊、光纖通訊、夜視鏡、保全監控、太
空、軍事、生物醫學、資料傳輸、雲端試算等，光通訊 PD 磊晶為近期主要成長動能。

註：A = 未調整之收盤價；B = 未調整之目標價； C = 依據股本變動調整後之目標價。員工分紅稀釋影響未反映於 A、B 或 C。

投資評等說明 
買進：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持正面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中增持該股。 
持有-超越同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力高於同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
持有-落後同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力低於同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
賣出：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持負面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中減持該股。 
評估中：本中心之預估、評等、目標價尚在評估中，但仍積極追蹤該個股。 
限制評等：為遵循相關法令規章及/或元大之政策，暫不給予評等及目標價。

註：元大給予個股之目標價係依 12 個月投資期間計算。大中華探索系列報告並無正式之 12 個月目標價，其投資建議乃根據分析師報告中之指定期間分析
而得。

The investment environment remains hostile. Lingering
uncertainty over the US tech story, Fed tightening and now a
little more tariﬀ uncertainty are leading to a full unwind of risk-
seeking trades, particularly in EMFX. More pressure could come
today with hot core US PCE inﬂation. Expect EMFX and
commodity FX to remain on the back foot

Chris Turner, Frantisek Taborsky and Francesco Pesole
Latin American currencies have occupied four of the ﬁve worst-performing
currency spots since 10 November
USD: EMFX unwind sends funds back to
USD
If the investment environment wasn't hostile enough already,
yesterday's Wall Street Journal report that the US Administration
was about to unleash a new set of tariﬀs on trading partners has
not helped the mood. These tariﬀs are expected to follow Section
301 investigations into excess manufacturing capacity overseas
and could add tariﬀs of around 5-10% on manufactured exports
from China, the EU and 14 other trading partners. This comes as

the Fed appears intent on pushing policy rates higher, while

Commodity currencies and EM currencies are getting hit. AUD/USD

in the month is a negative for procyclical currencies and a broad
dollar positive.
We said yesterday that the dollar may have overshot – and that
certainly is the case relative to short-term rate diﬀerentials. Yet the
continuing unwinding out of EM and commodity currencies is a big
dollar positive. Unless today's US inﬂation data can somehow
surprise on the downside, it looks like big resistance in DXY at

104.60 can be tested. Overall, expect a continued preference for a
basket of dollars, yen and Swiss francs as defensive currencies.
Chris Turner

dollar. There is also still a modest risk premium in the euro on the
back of French election risk. Even though the OAT-Bund spread is a

little narrower this week, we doubt that a move towards a 'golden

CEE: Local calendar getting more muted
Following the release of Polish data on Monday and Tuesday, the
CEE calendar is much quieter. The Polish data point to a dovish side
for the central bank. Notably, MPC member Ireneusz Dąbrowski
commented that the October ﬁgures support keeping rates
unchanged at the December meeting, suggesting that the current
level of interest rates is suﬃciently restrictive. This aligns with our

call that the National Bank of Poland will not hike rates until the ﬁrst
quarter of next year.
Rates across the region showed a mixed picture yesterday, though
there are signs of stabilisation. Meanwhile, FX failed to strengthen
despite a rebound in EUR/USD, with EUR/PLN and EUR/HUF moving
back higher. As we discussed over the past few days, the global
outlook has not shown much improvement, and bearish factors for
CEE currencies persist.
Locally, we see little room for further dovish pricing in Poland or the

pair. If the Czech National Bank maintains its hawkish tone
alongside rising inﬂation in the coming months, we could see a
short-term rebound. However, in the absence of hawkish central

Frantisek Taborsky
Latam: In harm's way
Latin American currencies have occupied four of the ﬁve worst-

performing currency spots since 10 November. That was when the
Fed's Chris Waller ﬂoated the idea of a 50bp rate hike and triggered
this broad correction in risk assets. The Colombian peso has fallen
close to 10%, reversing earlier gains on its status as one of the
world's most popular carry trades, backed by oil and ﬁscal
consolidation. The Brazilian real has been undermined by the ﬁscal
concerns of re-elected President Lula, Chile's peso hit by the copper

rout, and Mexico's peso left awfully exposed by Banxico's rate cuts.
Brazil's real tried to rally yesterday on news of extra ﬁscal
consolidation, yet the rally quickly petered out given the size of the
proposed spending cuts was small and the global EMFX sell-oﬀ
dominated.
We are reasonably constructive on the currencies of Brazil and Chile
into next year, but remain cautious about the Mexican peso.
Banxico might implicitly be welcoming a weaker peso, and we think

it will be slow to recover when the dollar ﬁnally turns lower. That
may not be until the second quarter of next year.
Chris Turner

## Document 336: Global Economics Analyst_ Macro Outlook 2027_ Steady Baseline, Downside Risks

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Economics Analyst_ Macro Outlook 2027_ Steady Baseline, Downside Risks.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 30
Extraction status: ok

### Page 2

Macro Outlook 2027: Steady Baseline, Downside Risks

We entered 2026 expecting sturdy global growth and slower inflation. The war in Iran 
derailed this inflation forecast, but the global economy proved more resilient than 
feared at the start of the war. 
Even though global headline inflation is on course to end 2026 at 3.7% on a Q4/Q4 
basis—1.3pp higher than our forecast from last year—we estimate that the global 
economy still grew at a remarkably unremarkable 2.7%, in line with potential and only 
0.1pp below our projections from our 2026 outlook (Exhibit 1). Our interpretation is that 
surging AI capex, easier financial conditions, and (in the US, Germany, and parts of Asia) 
fiscal expansion mostly offset the energy supply shock. As a result of these tailwinds, 
GDP growth only modestly underperformed in the US (-0.3pp), Euro area (-0.3pp), and 
China (-0.2pp) while significantly outperforming in Asian economies such as Taiwan 
(+8.6pp) and Korea (+1.9pp) that benefited from surging AI exports.

Growth Remains Solid but Unremarkable in 2027 
We expect another year of solid but unremarkable GDP growth in 2027, but several of 
the impulses that shaped global growth in 2026 will look different. 
The headwind from the energy supply shock will likely flip next year as energy prices 
normalize. Our commodity strategists’ baseline is that continued Middle East supply 
adaptation will lower Brent prices to $78/bbl by end-2027 with a similar normalization 
in global LNG prices (Exhibit 2). Based on these projections, we forecast that the energy 
price impulse to global growth will swing from a 0.4pp drag on global growth in 2026 to 
a modest boost in 2027 (although we see a continued drag in Europe from delayed 
energy cost pass-through to consumers).

A positive growth impulse from energy prices should outweigh the effect of tighter 
financial conditions and a smaller fiscal impulse in 2027. Our GS Financial Conditions 
Index (GS FCI) has tightened by around 50bp in the US and by 30bp in developed 
markets (DMs) over the last three months on the back of lower equity prices and higher 
rates. It now implies a moderate drag on growth over the next few quarters (Exhibit 3). 
We do not expect meaningful fiscal consolidation owing to higher rates in 2027, but the 
large fiscal expansions of the last few years are fading. The main exception is Japan, 
where consumption tax cuts, an increase in defense spending, and the Takaichi 
administration’s investment plans point to a meaningful 0.7pp boost to GDP growth. We 
also expect modestly positive impulses in Germany and the US—where the Democratic 
victory in November’s US election suggests some of the spending cuts from the 2025 
One Big Beautiful Bill Act will be reversed—but a continued fiscal drag in the UK that 
leaves the DM fiscal impulse only slightly positive.

The main global growth impulse that we do not expect to change in 2027 is increased AI 
capex. We forecast that nominal global AI investment will rise from $1.1tn in 2026 to 
$1.8tn in 2027, mostly driven by an increase of over $500bn in hardware spending in the 
US (Exhibit 4). We expect the investment surge to be frontloaded, with the 
year-over-year change in investment as a share of GDP peaking in 2027H1 (the level of 
investment as a share of GDP peaks in 2028 under our forecast). Even with the expected 
slowdown, the impact on GDP growth will be sizable: we estimate that AI investment will 
boost GDP growth by 0.4pp globally. And although we again estimate only a small 0.2pp 
boost to measured US GDP growth in 2027 owing to continued undercounting of 
semiconductor investment and chip-related intellectual property exports, we estimate a 
larger 0.6pp boost to “true” GDP growth after correcting for these measurement biases.

The other notable growth impulse from 2026 that will continue in 2027 is a drag on

of GDP property sector drag and weak underlying consumer spending, resulting in a 
modest 0.1pp headwind to GDP growth for the rest of the world, with larger growth 
drags in manufacturing-heavy economies such as Germany (-0.3pp) and Central Europe 
(-0.2pp).

### Page 6

Taken together, our 2027 global GDP growth forecast is modestly optimistic at 2.8%, a 
pace that is 0.1pp above our estimate of potential. As has been the case for the last 
several years, we are more optimistic on US growth (2.4% vs. 2.2% consensus) owing to 
resilient investment and more downbeat on the European growth outlook (0.9% vs. 
1.3%) owing to tighter financial conditions and our expectation that higher energy 
prices will start to bite this winter. We also expect continued robust growth in South 
Korea (3.2% vs. 2.7%) and Taiwan (6.3% vs. 5.9%) reflecting another year of strong AI 
exports.

Downside Risks 
We see risks around our baseline global growth forecasts as skewed to the downside for 
three reasons. 
1. A Renewed Energy Shock 
The first and most immediate risk is that an escalation of the war in Iran could lead to 
more significant energy price increases, creating growth headwinds that—unlike in 
2026—FCI and fiscal impulses would not offset in 2027. While our commodity strategists 
see risks around their energy price baselines as two-sided, they put more weight on an 
upside scenario where intensified shipping attacks, damage to oil infrastructure, and a 
more persistent risk premium push oil prices up to $132/bbl (Exhibit 7). If these risks 
materialize, the energy price impulse would remain a moderate drag on global GDP 
growth in 2027.

2. Bond Market Turbulence 
Second, we worry about a bigger fiscal risk premium. In 2026, we saw the consequences 
of large-scale deficits—coupled with AI-linked borrowing and central bank hikes—show 
up more clearly in higher longer-term interest rates. Although rates have eased 
somewhat over the last few weeks, the DM yield curve remains 85bp above its level in 
early 2026. If sustained, we estimate that this increase would subtract a cumulative 0.5% 
from GDP via weakness in housing, consumer durables, and business investment (Exhibit 
8). Only part of this drag is incorporated in our standing forecasts because we expect 
bond yields to pull back as central banks deliver fewer hikes than priced in markets.

3. A Weaker AI Impulse
The transition to an AI-enabled economy is occurring in two phases. The first phase
involves an investment boom that raises both corporate profits and global GDP growth
but features only limited productivity gains and labor market disruption (because it
takes firms several years to adopt AI and reorganize to take full advantage of the new
technology). The second phase involves lower hardware investment but more visible
business reorganization, as well as more substantial productivity gains and labor market

disruption.
To illustrate this transition, we overlay a simulated AI capex path with our estimates of
the labor market impacts under a 10-year adoption timeline (Exhibit 10). The capex
path assumes that US AI investment follows equity analysts’ projections through 2032,
after which the capital stock converges toward a long-run equilibrium level.1  Under our
baseline calibration, the AI capital stock eventually converges to a long-run level of
around 9% of GDP, and 9% of total US workers are displaced. (We continue to expect

most of these displaced workers to find new jobs, including in new occupations that are
created by AI).
Under these assumptions, the transition from the first phase to the second phase
doesn’t occur until 2028-2029, when we see a further pickup in the underlying US
productivity trend to the 2½-3% range and a gradual increase in the US unemployment
rate to 4½%. A backloaded transition is consistent with the projections by equity
analysts that AI investment will keep growing through 2028, albeit at a slower pace than

in 2026-2027. It is also consistent with the renewed pickup in US employment growth
since the spring, which has temporarily laid to rest concerns around imminent AI-related
job market disruption.
1 We characterize the long-run equilibrium level following Hall and Jorgenson (1967) as a function of the
depreciation rate, technological cost disinflation, nominal interest rates, and the AI capital factor share of total
output.
12 November 2026   9
Goldman Sachs Global Economics Analyst

### Page 10

But it is hard to be sure about the timing of this shift and we see some signs that it could 
occur earlier.  Hyperscaler equity price reactions to earnings call signals for higher capex 
switched from positive in 2024-2025 to slightly negative in 2026 as the market has 
become concerned about overinvestment (Exhibit 11). Furthermore, upward revisions to 
hyperscaler capex slowed in the Q3 earnings season, which suggests that the AI 
investment cycle peak is shifting more clearly into view.

### Page 11

Beyond the fundamental demand for compute, we see three other near-term downside 
risks for AI investment: 
Capacity constraints – Semiconductor and memory producers are capacity n
constrained with order backlogs through 2028. While capacity investments currently 
underway should eventually alleviate these constraints, a surge in nominal 
investment in 2027 may increasingly raise prices (thereby pushing central banks 
toward more hawkish policy) without boosting real output. 
Credit markets – Global AI-related debt issuance has totaled $687bn so far in 2026 n
while AI-related equity issuance has added another $115bn. While our market 
strategists see adequate capital supply to meet even higher capital demand next 
year, upward pressure on borrowing costs could lead to more meaningful crowd-out 
of other investment in 2027 (vs. only modest crowd-out in 2026). 
Regulation – Concerns regarding regulatory headwinds to investment surged after n
the US midterm elections in early November raised the possibility of data center 
permitting moratoria in several states (particularly in Texas). We do not anticipate 
that regulation will derail 2027 investment, both because moratoria will likely be 
short-lived and because permits for 2027 projects have mostly already been issued. 
But a more extended delay or stringent regulatory shift would raise downside risks to 
AI investment in 2028-2029, which could weigh on growth in 2027 via tighter 
financial conditions. 
Turning to the labor market, we have argued that AI adoption is likely to displace jobs 
and cause a temporary increase in the unemployment rate of around ½ a percentage 
point over the next several years.2  Our baseline expectation is that this increase occurs 
in a backloaded fashion, with only a small effect in 2027 and more notable effects in 
2028-2029.  However, we are starting to see signs that the AI impact is spreading

beyond technology to the broader economy. Across nonmanufacturing industries, the 
share of US firms that have adopted AI in regular business functions now shows a 
statistically significant negative correlation with employment growth and a statistically 
significant positive correlation with unemployment (correlations were insignificant at the 
start of the year, Exhibit 12).

Moreover, AI-driven job displacement would likely accelerate in a downside growth 
scenario. Exhibit 13 shows that the trend decline in the employment share of routine 
occupations over the past five decades has been highly concentrated in recessions.  
Given that AI has sharply increased the number of occupations exposed to automation, 
we would expect corporate leaders to respond to a slowdown by reducing headcount 
targets more aggressively, putting more upward pressure on the unemployment rate.

### Page 13

To be clear, we still expect AI-related pressure on the labor market to remain temporary.  
Employment in existing industries such as healthcare is likely to keep growing rapidly, 
and history shows that technological revolutions ultimately result in the creation of new 
industries and occupations that absorb workers displaced elsewhere.  Historically, this 
dynamism has kept the unemployment rate from increasing permanently despite all the 
labor-saving technological innovations of the past 250 years.  But over the next few 
years, job market disruption is likely to grow.  
Inflation: Progress Not Perfection 
Inflation progress was slower than expected in 2026 owing to price increases associated 
with the war in Iran.  But under our baseline commodity forecasts for a steady decline in 
energy prices next year, inflation should slow in 2027. 
The case for disinflation is particularly strong in the US because the price boosts from 
tariffs (which are still adding 40bp to year-over-year core PCE inflation through 
October) should fully drop out of the year-over-year inflation statistics next spring 
(Exhibit 14). Combined with a much smaller inflation boost from the war in Iran, we 
expect core PCE to slow from 3.2% at end-2026 to 2.4% at end-2027.

Exhibit 13: Prior Automation Cycles Suggest That Aggregate Automation Speeds Up in

### Page 14

Despite our expectation for progress, we see several reasons why inflation may remain 
somewhat firm in early 2027, thereby delaying a return to target-consistent levels in 
most economies. 
First, while most of the price boost from higher energy prices has been realized, some 
pass-through will spill into next year (Exhibit 15).  This is true both in the US and 
especially in Europe, where indirect effects have so far been very limited.  For example, 
European airfares have not repriced higher in response to fuel cost increases so far—
reflecting fuel hedges, greater competition, and pre-war plans for supply 
expansion—but should pick up notably next year.

### Page 15

Second, global memory price forecasts were again revised higher over the last earnings 
season on the back of robust demand (Exhibit 16).  This pattern both suggests more 
near-term upside to core inflation from AI spillovers—although we still anticipate price 
increases should slow as production capacity increases—and points to upside risks if 
recent pricing trends continue.  This is especially true in the US, where the impact from 
higher memory prices is exaggerated because the weight of the software and 
accessories category is overstated.

Third, upstream cost pressures have increased and broadened (Exhibit 17). These 
increases are accounted for in our baseline forecasts, but historical patterns suggest that 
the recent rise in upstream cost pressures will keep inflation surprise risks elevated 
through the spring. We are particularly concerned about a reacceleration in the US, 
where the “January effect” has driven a seasonal inflation pickup almost every year since 
the pandemic (our forecast incorporates a 0.1pp boost to January core PCE).

We still see most of these price pressures as backward-looking and temporary, however, 
and remain less worried about a sustained inflation overshoot.  We take particular 
comfort in the downward trend in wage growth in most major economies that lowers the 
risk of a pickup in core services inflation (Exhibit 18).  Furthermore, leading indicators 
continue to point toward an incremental softening in US shelter inflation, and we have 
yet to see signs of second-round effects in Europe.

Taken together, we forecast a steady decline in year-over-year core inflation in the US 
and a more delayed decline in other major economies once the energy price shock is 
fully digested (Exhibit 19).  And although core inflation remains somewhat above target 
at end-2027 on a year-over-year basis, we expect that the inflation constraint will

increasingly become less binding in most economies as sequential inflation eases over 
the course of the year.

The largest risk to our near-term inflation view is that any renewed escalation in Iran 
could prolong headline (and to some degree core) inflation pressures.  Applying our 
standard rules of thumb to our commodity team’s adverse scenario implies 0.9pp upside 
to headline inflation and 0.2pp upside to core inflation relative to our baseline forecasts 
for 2027. 
Still Expecting Fewer Hikes Than Priced 
We forecast incremental policy tightening through early 2027 in most major economies 
(and see a low bar for modest incremental tightening in others), but our economic 
forecasts support a dovish view on rates in 2027: central banks could either deliver 
gradual normalization cuts later in the year as inflation comes down or pivot to cuts 
sooner if the downside growth risks discussed above materialize. 
In the US, we expect the Fed to deliver rate hikes in December and March in response to 
lingering inflation pressures (Exhibit 20).  But softer inflation data should allow the Fed 
to cut once in December 2027, followed by three more cuts in 2028 to a terminal rate of 
3.25-3.5%.  Furthermore, we see more dovish rate risks in the US than in most other 
economies, both because US inflation drivers look more clearly temporary and because 
the US is most exposed to a downturn in the AI cycle.

Exhibit 19: Our Inflation Forecasts Remain Benign

### Page 18

Our views on other major DMs also skew dovish (Exhibit 21). We expect the ECB to 
deliver its final hike in December, before starting to normalize back toward a 2.25% 
terminal rate in 2027Q4. And although higher-than-expected inflation could prompt 
another hike in Q1, our softer European growth outlook limits policy rate upside. In the 
UK, we expect a final BoE hike to 4.25% in February before a hold for the remainder of 
2027. We likewise expect normalization cuts from the BoE to a 3.25% terminal rate, but 
not until 2028. Our ECB and BoE forecasts are dovish relative to market pricing on both a 
baseline and a risk-adjusted basis. 
We are also dovish on Canada and Australia. In Canada, we expect two hikes to 3.0% in 
the first half of next year based on recent BoC communication suggesting it plans to 
return to the center of its neutral range (which we believe will be revised up to 2.5-3.5% 
in April).  But with trade uncertainty looming large, it is more likely that the BoC 
underdelivers than hikes above the midpoint of its neutral range (as the market is 
pricing).  In Australia, our RBA forecast for a hold at 4.85% through October is broadly in 
line with market pricing over 2027H1, but more dovish in 2027H2 and especially by 
end-2028 (by which time we expect a cumulative 125bp in cuts to have been delivered).  
The main outlier to our forecast of only modest further policy tightening is Japan. We 
expect the BoJ to deliver another 25bp hike at the December MPM, reflecting recent 
BoJ communication highlighting both upside inflation risks and the impact of higher 
global interest rates on the Yen. But with core CPI (ex fresh food but including energy) 
likely to rise above 3.5% through next summer, we now expect that the BoJ will follow 
through with additional hikes in March and June for a 2.0% terminal rate.

Our views also skew dovish in EMs (Exhibit 22). This is particularly true in CEEMEA and 
LatAm, where we expect most central banks to cut through 2027.  In Asia, we expect 
rates to stay flat or rise slightly (though our forecasts are still below market pricing 
except in Taiwan). Energy prices are the main upside risk to EM rates, but we think 
markets overestimate the inflation risk in most EMs for three reasons. First, inflation was 
already low before the energy shock. Second, EM economies are cyclically weaker than 
DMs. Third, EM currencies have held up well, supported by healthy external balances. 
Some EMs may tighten further over the next few quarters, but we expect most to hold or 
cut after that.

### Page 20

More Challenges for Markets than the Macro
As with the economic outlook, the market outlook continues to be dominated by the
path of the AI cycle and energy risks related to the Iran war.
Our baseline forecast is quite friendly. The mix of steady growth, falling core inflation,
gradually moderating energy prices and an earlier peak in G10 hiking cycles than market
pricing does not signal obvious threats to asset markets. The clearest difference with

market pricing is our more benign view of the policy rate path for many economies,
which all else equal should prove a boost to both equities and bonds. Our central asset
forecasts look for positive returns across the major asset classes. After the recent
sell-off, our forecasts imply robust equity returns between now and the end of 2027, but
we expect the pace of gains to slow as we move through next year.
Despite this baseline, we think the market outlook will continue to prove challenging, as

it has been already for several months. In part, this is because of the risks around our
baseline that we have already described. In part, it is because we think markets—
especially around the AI theme—have been priced in ways that leave them more
vulnerable to signs that important tailwinds may be starting to fade. In the process, the
volatility and rotations that have mostly remained under the surface of major equity and
credit markets this year could spill over more into shifts at the index level.

Managing the Peak in the AI Investment Cycle
The biggest uncertainties—as with the economic outlook—lie around the AI cycle. With
AI investment growth expected to remain robust, the peak in the AI investment impulse
should be a manageable event for the economy. But it is already proving to be a bigger
test for markets for three reasons. First, the AI impulse has been a much larger
contributor to growth in earnings, market value and capital spending than to growth in

GDP. Second, AI-related equities have taken credit for significant macro productivity
gains, especially companies benefiting directly from the infrastructure boom, that have
made them more vulnerable to any signs that we are on a less optimistic path. Third, the
point at which growth rates peak (a “second derivative” turning point) often matters,
even if underlying growth rates (the “first derivative”) remain healthy. Moving through
that peak allows markets to place limits on the upside of an uncertain distribution and as

growth decelerates, the period of positive surprises to expectations often ends too, as
we may be starting to see for the AI capex cycle.
12 November 2026   20
Goldman Sachs Global Economics Analyst

### Page 21

Markets have already been reflecting these concerns. Since June, we have seen a 
significant derating of companies levered to the AI infrastructure theme, which are most 
sensitive to shifts in the investment outlook and had been the biggest market 
beneficiaries in the first half of 2026. The good news is that robust profits, and a rising 
share of income going to listed companies, have helped keep both the imbalances and 
the deep valuation excesses that we saw in the late 1990s at bay. The key question in this 
cycle is whether the market is correctly assessing how much of the recent acceleration in 
profits, which has taken S&P 500 earnings to a record high relative to GDP, will prove 
persistent especially as the investment cycle itself fades. Without the capital inflows 
from the EM crises in 1997 and 1998—or the rate cuts that goods price deflation 
allowed—increased financing needs have also been putting clearer upward pressure on 
the cost of capital.

### Page 22

A Flatter Trend, but Risks of a Bumpier Transition 
Two things give us comfort in terms of how bumpy this transition will prove to be. The 
first is that although the investment impulse from AI is poised to peak, growth in both AI 
investment and earnings should remain firmly positive in 2027, especially earlier in the 
year. We expect S&P 500 earnings growth to decelerate from 36% in 2026 to a 
still-robust 11% in 2027. This may help to limit the de-rating of even the most directly 
affected areas and should reduce the impact on broader growth-sensitive assets. This is 
particularly true since recent anxiety about AI-related returns has pushed the valuations 
of many technology companies lower. The second is that the baton may be passed from 
the direct beneficiaries of the AI investment boom to other areas of the market. This 
includes other areas of the AI complex, such as the hyperscalers, if monetization paths 
become more visible; companies which may begin to show benefits from AI productivity 
enhancements; or non-AI companies which see some relief as the crowding out of 
capital available to them eases (and are now outperforming the broad AI complex in 
2026). 
The risk is that what we expect to be an eventual flattening in the upward trend in asset 
markets—and a rotation away from some of the largest recent winners—becomes a 
bigger aggregate shock. This could happen because the market begins to look further 
forward to the peak in the investment share itself (in 2028 on our forecasts); because 
the passing of the baton to other sectors does not take place as quickly or as smoothly 
as needed; because a fresh energy spike puts non-AI areas back under pressure; or 
because feedback loops from financial and credit conditions accelerate more quickly 
than in our baseline. We think the risk to markets is higher than the risk to the economic 
picture, because the boom itself has had its largest impact there. But tighter financial 
conditions—both in equities and rates—are the most obvious channel through which the 
economic impact could also increase.

Exhibit 24: S&P 500 Earnings Are at a Record High Relative to GDP

### Page 23

A Gradual Shift from Rate Risk to Growth Risk
Our inflation and policy views remain more distinctive than our growth views. With our
central bank views generally more dovish than the markets, particularly as we move
deeper into 2027, our baseline forecast implies scope for front-end pricing to fall
further. The mix of high fiscal deficits and the ongoing AI capex boom limit the scope for
longer-dated yields to fall in our baseline. But if policy rates peak at the lower levels we

expect, forward rates should also move lower (we see the US 10-year yield at 4.6% at
the end of 2027). Faster energy relief would accentuate that process.
This kind of policy relief would be helpful for both equities and bonds. But shifts in the
nature of the risks facing asset markets may be starting to alter the balance of risks
between equities and bonds, and correlations across asset markets, as we move into
2027. Through much of 2026, global bonds failed to protect equity portfolios. The

inflationary impulse from energy disruptions and the surprising resilience of growth and
labor markets in the face of those shocks prompted a sharp shift in policy rate
expectations. The start of a broad-based G10 hiking cycle has pushed real rates sharply
higher in the last 9 months. Under the surface, the mix of rising corporate sector
financing needs with still-high government deficits helped to underpin the upward
pressure on global real yields.

In the near term, the key risks to our baseline economic forecasts still push in that
direction. The most obvious is that any escalation in the Iran war could push energy
prices sharply higher again in the coming months. Particularly after the fall in the risk
premium in oil markets coming into November, a fresh energy shock would hurt equities,
support the US dollar and put renewed upward pressure on rates markets. Recent
firmness in core inflation, and Fed commentary, are also likely to keep worries about a

more front-loaded hiking cycle alive. The impulse from AI investment is also arguably
moving through its strongest point, which may limit any decline in yields.
But the further we move into 2027, the more likely it is that the key risks are positive for
bonds, as the threats from central bank hikes, energy price spikes and the AI investment
tailwind recede and other risks become more prominent. A sharper reversal in the AI
boom or earlier AI-related labor market disruption are the most obvious versions of that

story. But the kinds of financial stresses from a high-rate environment that we saw
emerge in September and October—most obviously in France heading towards the April
election but potentially also elsewhere—would push in a similar direction, as would
“black swan” risks such as a major cybersecurity event.
12 November 2026   23
Goldman Sachs Global Economics Analyst

### Page 24

Diversification and Protection as the Market Cycle Matures 
With a steady economic backdrop, our baseline forecasts for 2027 are for positive 
returns in equities and bonds, and close to zero returns in commodities. But with a 
maturing market cycle and significant uncertainty around core drivers of the outlook, we 
continue to emphasize the value of diversification and hedges to protect against key 
risks.

We expect ongoing earnings growth alongside the recent valuation compression to 
support further gains in major equity markets in 2027. As the pace of earnings growth 
slows moving into 2027, the pace of US equity gains is likely to drop too, moving well 
below the more than 20% annualized rate of the last three years. With earnings growth

### Page 25

likely to be slower still in 2028, the risk is that the market pulls forward its worries about
a deeper turn in the cycle. Accounting for risks around the baseline view, we recommend
an overweight allocation in the US and Japan, neutral Asia ex Japan/EM, and
underweight in Europe.
We expect further widening in credit spreads in 2027 as the increase in corporate supply
continues. Although widening is modest in our base case, risks are clearly skewed in that

direction. It is hard to see scenarios where US credit spreads tighten sharply (there is
more scope for tightening in Europe where the risk premium has risen more), and easier
to see how spreads could move higher, particularly if economic conditions deteriorate
more than we expect or if investors become more uncomfortable about the profitability
of the AI investment boom. We expect US HY to underperform, with some risk that
problems in the tail of weaker borrowers create more turbulent conditions for broader

financing. Without the large capital inflows and rate cuts prompted by the EM crises in
1997-98, the sustained divergence between credit and equity markets from 1998 to
2000 may more challenging now.
Government bonds offer better returns than in recent years, reflecting higher yields and
improving relative valuations. Real bond yields are at high levels compared with the last
30 years, both absolutely and relative to equities.  Our baseline is for bonds to deliver a

little more than the decent running yield that they currently offer, though the scope for
capital gains is likely to be higher later in 2027. There are risks to that view from fresh
energy shocks, or stickier core inflation than we expect. But if clearer downside risks to
growth emerge, yields could fall more clearly.
After tight ranges in 2026, our baseline is for modest USD weakness as lower inflation
and energy put the USD on the back foot, alongside further appreciation in CNY

underpinned by the surging trade surplus. But if the market challenges US tech
exceptionalism more quickly, we could see a sharper shift away from the USD. And the
same set of growth risks that are increasing the hedging value of bonds would also likely
prompt a sharper appreciation in the Japanese Yen.
Commodities continue to offer value as a portfolio hedge. Despite the ongoing war with
Iran, oil market supply has largely been restored, but risks of escalation and further

disruptions have led to a large but variable risk premium above the oil price justified by
the fundamental supply-demand balance. Our baseline is that this risk premium will
slowly erode, leaving energy and core commodity returns more muted. But there is still
heightened risk of scenarios where energy prices could move much higher, hurting
bonds and equities. While there is also a downside tail to energy prices, if there is faster
progress towards a resolution of the war with Iran, in that scenario equities and bonds

would likely be performing well.
12 November 2026   25
Goldman Sachs Global Economics Analyst

### Page 26

Alongside diversification, direct hedges are worth considering. Positioning for lower 
rates in the US and Japan, or for jointly lower rates and equities are potential ways to 
protect against deeper downside growth tails. In FX, USD/JPY downside may play a 
similar role, given that it generally performs well when rates and equities are both falling. 
For energy upside, hedges in European gas futures and TTF calls look like asymmetric 
ways to protect against risk. So far, the sharpest volatility has been under the surface of 
the major equity indices. Equity index volatility has risen a little but is unlikely to fall 
much from here and we think has scope to rise further if macro stresses broaden. 
Deeper protection in equities themselves—either through out-of-the-money puts or 
replacing equity holdings with longer-dated call options—also seems more valuable 
than usual as the cycle matures.

ANALYST: Tajinder Dhillon, CFA October 30, 2026 
tajinder.dhillon@lseg.com; 
THIS WEEK IN EARNINGS 
AGGREGATE ESTIMATES AND REVISIONS 
 26Q3 Y/Y earnings are expected to be 31.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the Y/Y earnings estimate is 26.4%. 
 Of the 299 companies in the S&P 500 that have reported earnings to date for 26Q3, 81.6% have reported earnings 
above analyst estimates. This compares to a long-term average of 67.7% and prior four quarter average of 81.5%. 
 26Q3 Y/Y revenue is expected to be 13.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the growth estimate is 12.2%. 
 69.9% of companies have reported 26Q3 revenue above analyst expectations. This compares to a long-term 
average of 63% and an average over the past four quarters of 76.8%. 
 For 26Q3, there have been 40 negative EPS preannouncements issued by S&P 500 corporations compared to 66 
positive EPS preannouncements. By dividing 40 by 66 the N/P ratio is 0.6 for the S&P 500 Index.  
 The forward four-quarter (26Q4– 27Q3) P/E ratio for the S&P 500 is 19.0. 
 During the week of Nov. 2, 144 S&P 500 companies are expected to report quarterly earnings.

There has been an increase in the share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 since the start of the quarter (to $778.4B 
from $760.7B). Three of the eleven sectors have experienced downward revisions to estimates.

Since Aug. 1, the communication services (-20.5%) and materials (-7%) sectors have recorded the highest percentage 
decreases in earnings, while the energy (33%) sector has recorded the highest percentage increase in earnings. 
Overall, share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 2.3% since the start of the quarter.

Since Aug. 1, the communication services (-14.7B) and materials (-1.2B) sectors have recorded the highest dollar-level 
decreases in earnings, while the energy (15.7B) sector has recorded the highest dollar-level increase in earnings. 
Overall, expected share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 17.7B since the start of the quarter.

299 companies in the S&P 500 Index have reported earnings for Q3 2026. Of these companies, 81.6% reported 
earnings above analyst expectations and 15.7% reported earnings below analyst expectations. In a typical quarter 
(since 1994), 67% of companies beat estimates and 20% miss estimates. Over the past four quarters, 82% of 
companies beat the estimates and 15% missed estimates. 
In aggregate, companies are reporting earnings that are 4.5% above estimates, which compares to a long-term (since 
1994) average surprise factor of 4.4% and the average surprise factor over the prior four quarters of 7.7%.

The estimated earnings growth rate for the S&P 500 for 26Q3 is 31.6%. If the energy sector is excluded, the growth 
rate declines to 26.4%. The S&P 500 expects to see share-weighted earnings of $778.4B in 26Q3, compared to share-
weighted earnings of $591.4B (based on the year-ago earnings of the current 503 constituents) in 25Q3. 
11 of the 11 sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in earnings relative to 25Q3. The Energy and 
Information Technology sectors have the highest earnings growth rates for the quarter, while the Consumer 
Discretionary sector has the weakest anticipated growth compared to 25Q3. 
The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (146.2%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $63B in 26Q3, 
compared to earnings of $25.6B in 25Q3. 4 of the 5 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see higher earnings 
than a year ago. The Oil & Gas Refining & Marketing (566.5%) and Integrated Oil & Gas (143.8%) sub-industries have 
the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate declines to 39%. 
The Information Technology sector has the second highest earnings growth rate (68.9%) of any sector. It is expected 
to earn $260.2B in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $154B in 25Q3. 12 of the 12 sub-industries in the sector are 
anticipated to see higher earnings than a year ago. The Semiconductors (137%) and Semiconductor Materials & 
Equipment (71.5%) sub-industries have the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, 
the growth rate declines to 28.2%. 
The Consumer Discretionary sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (3.9%) of any sector. It is expected to earn 
$56.3B in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $54.2B in 25Q3. 7 of the 18 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to 
see lower earnings than a year ago. The Casinos & Gaming (-44.1%) and Automotive Parts & Equipment (-38.8%) 
sub-industries have the lowest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate 
improves to 4.5%.

The estimated earnings growth rate for the S&P 500 for 26Q4 is 29.7%. If the energy sector is excluded, the growth 
rate declines to 25%. The S&P 500 expects to see share-weighted earnings of $810.7B in 26Q4, compared to share-
weighted earnings of $625.1B (based on the year-ago earnings of the current 503 constituents) in 25Q4. 
11 of the 11 sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in earnings relative to 25Q4. The Energy and 
Information Technology sectors have the highest earnings growth rates for the quarter, while the Industrials sector has 
the weakest anticipated growth compared to 25Q4. 
The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (145.6%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $59.5B in 26Q4, 
compared to earnings of $24.2B in 25Q4. 3 of the 5 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see higher earnings 
than a year ago. The Oil & Gas Refining & Marketing (366.3%) and Integrated Oil & Gas (175.7%) sub-industries have 
the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate declines to 52.1%. 
The Information Technology sector has the second highest earnings growth rate (56.3%) of any sector. It is expected 
to earn $292.1B in 26Q4, compared to earnings of $186.8B in 25Q4. 12 of the 12 sub-industries in the sector are 
anticipated to see higher earnings than a year ago. The Semiconductors (119.5%) and Semiconductor Materials & 
Equipment (72.3%) sub-industries have the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, 
the growth rate declines to 19.1%. 
The Industrials sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (3.9%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $52B in 26Q4, 
compared to earnings of $50.1B in 25Q4. 2 of the 20 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see lower earnings 
than a year ago. The Aerospace & Defense (-39.6%) and Passenger Airlines (-29.8%) sub-industries have the lowest 
earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate improves to 30.5%.

500 expects to see revenue of $4898.8B in 26Q3, compared to revenue of $4310.4B (based on the year-ago earnings of the current 503 
constituents) in 25Q3.

11 of the 11 sectors anticipate revenue growth for the quarter. The Information Technology sector has the highest revenue growth rate for the 
quarter, while the Financials sector has the weakest anticipated growth compared to 25Q3.

The Information Technology sector has the highest revenue growth rate (40.7%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $772.3B in 26Q3, compared to 
revenue of $548.8B in 25Q3. 12 of the 12 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see higher revenue than a year ago. The Semiconductors 
(88%) and Semiconductor Materials & Equipment (49.4%) sub-industries have the highest revenue growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are 
removed, the growth rate declines to 23.7%.

The Financials sector has the lowest revenue growth rate (5.1%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $576.7B in 26Q3, compared to revenue of 
$548.4B in 25Q3. 3 of the 13 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see lower revenue than a year ago. The Diversified Financial Services (-
40.9%) and Reinsurance (-18.1%) sub-industries have the lowest revenue growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate 
improves to 6%.

STARMINE EARNINGS SURPRISE FORECAST
Looking forward at quarterly performance, we use StarMine’s SmartEstimate® from LSEG to determine which
companies in the S&P 500 are better poised to beat and miss earnings estimates. The SmartEstimate® is a weighted

average of analyst estimates, with more weight given to more recent estimates and more accurate analysts. Our
studies have shown that when the SmartEstimate® differs significantly from the consensus (I/B/E/S mean estimate),
the Predicted Surprise accurately predicts the direction of earnings surprises or further revisions 70% of the time.
When a significant Predicted Surprise for revenue is also present for the period, the accuracy improves to 78%.

StarMine ARM from LSEG is an analyst revisions stock ranking model, designed to predict future changes in analyst
sentiment. ARM incorporates more accurate earnings estimates through the SmartEstimate prediction service. ARM
region rankings scores companies by region on a scale of 1 to 100 where 100 represents the most bullish sentiment.
Over the next two weeks, 147 S&P 500 companies are expected to report earnings. Of these companies, 17 positive

surprises and 11 negative surprises are expected from S&P 500 companies reporting quarterly results.

## Document 366: Meta将Muse智能体接入Instagram与沃尔玛

Source PDF: all report pdf/Meta将Muse智能体接入Instagram与沃尔玛.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-25
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

沃尔玛与 Meta 达成合作，⽤⼾可通过 AI 助⼿ Muse 搜索⿊五促销商品、⽐较价格并完成下
单，⽆需浏览沃尔玛⽹站。合作由沃尔玛 Agent Gateway 系统⽀持，该系统将向更多 AI 平
台开放。与此同时，沃尔玛持续升级⾃有助⼿ Sparky ，将 AI 功能延伸⾄线上购物和线下⻔
店导航。
摘要：
沃尔玛正尝试让消费者通过 AI 助⼿直接完成购物。当地时间周⼆，沃尔玛宣布与 Meta 达成合
作，⽤⼾可以通过 Meta 旗下 AI 智能体 Muse 购买沃尔玛的⿊⾊星期五促销商品，⽆需⾃⾏浏览沃
尔玛⽹站，即可完成商品搜索、⽐价和下单。
具体⽽⾔，⽤⼾打开 Muse 应⽤并登录沃尔玛账号后，只需描述所需商品， Muse 便会向沃尔玛
发送请求，由沃尔玛返回相关商品信息，帮助⽤⼾⽐较选项、寻找优惠并⽣成购物⻋。
沃尔玛表⽰，合作体验内置了隐私保护机制。Muse ⽆法访问⽤⼾的沃尔玛购物历史及其他个⼈
信息；消费者可以设置消费限额、要求购买前审批，并查看 AI 代理提交的订单，也可以随时删

除代理档案。
此次合作意味着沃尔玛开始通过外部 AI 助⼿拓展购物⼊⼝。⽤⼾不再必须进⼊零售商⾃有⽹站搜
索商品，⽽是可以将购物需求交给 AI 代理处理。
开放 Agent Gateway ，沃尔玛争夺 AI 购物新⼊⼝
此次合作由沃尔玛今年 10 ⽉上线的代理⽹关系统（ Agent Gateway ）提供⽀持。该系统允许外
部 AI 助⼿直接连接沃尔玛线上商店，获取商品信息并协助消费者购物。
沃尔玛计划向更多开发者和 AI 平台开放 Agent Gateway 。这意味着未来接⼊沃尔玛的 AI 助⼿可能
不⽌ Muse ，更多外部平台也有机会通过这⼀系统提供购物服务。 Instacart 近期也与 Meta 旗下
Muse 达成了合作。
沃尔玛与亚⻢逊在这⼀领域采取了不同策略。亚⻢逊已采取措施阻⽌第三⽅ AI 代理代替消费者完
成购买，⽽沃尔玛选择主动开放零售接⼝，允许外部 AI 助⼿参与商品搜索和交易流程。
两种路线反映出零售商对 AI 时代电商⼊⼝的不同判断：亚⻢逊倾向于维护⾃⾝平台的交易闭环，

沃尔玛则希望借助外部 AI 助⼿扩⼤商品触达范围，将其转化为新的购物渠道。
升级⾃有助⼿ Sparky ， AI 延伸⾄线下购物
在与外部 AI 平台合作的同时，沃尔玛也在升级⾃有购物助⼿ Sparky ，将 AI 应⽤延伸⾄实体⻔
店。
近期推出的新功能包括将⼿写购物清单转化为购物⻋、通过照⽚和语⾳指令搜索商品，以及帮
助消费者在⻔店内定位商品。⽤⼾搜索商品后，可以通过⻔店地图查看其所在货架； “ 闪现价签 ”
（ Flash Price Tag ）功能还可以让⽬标商品的货架价签亮起，⽅便消费者寻找。
从 Muse 接⼊外部购物场景，到 Sparky 覆盖线上搜索和线下寻货，沃尔玛正在将 AI 嵌⼊消费者购
物的不同环节。随着 Agent Gateway 向更多平台开放，外部 AI 助⼿能否为沃尔玛带来更多实际订
单，以及 AI 代理是否会逐渐成为电商购物的重要⼊⼝，将成为后续值得关注的⽅向。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Charts of the week: Watching earnings and a potential ‘Blue wave’

US Big Tech earnings, mid-term elections and major central bank policy meetings are the near-term market drivers 
Consensus S&P500 index Q3 earnings estimates by sector   Chance of Democrats winning control of US House and Senate*

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered; *Polymarket probability

Editorial 
Policy, politics and earnings  
Strategy summary: We retain our near-term defensive and 
diversified stance across assets, sectors and regions amid 
near-term uncertainty around monetary policy, politics and 
earnings. The next two weeks will be busy for major central 
banks, US political watchers and technology leaders powering 
the AI supercycle. We expect the Fed, ECB and BoJ to hold 
rates next week, but markets will focus on whether central 
banks are turning more hawkish due to the latest surge in fuel 
prices. The US mid-term elections are expected to give 
Democrats control of the House and Senate, with implications 
for taxes and the AI-led investment boom.  
Meanwhile, the consensus is expecting the US tech sector to 
report over 60% y/y earnings growth for Q3, the biggest jump 
in over two decades outside the post-COVID rebound.  Each of 
these three drivers have the potential to surprise markets.  
Big Three to hold monetary policy: We expect the Fed, ECB 
and BoJ to hold policy rates next week, after September’s 
hikes, as they assess whether higher bond yields have 
tightened financial conditions enough to offset fuel-price-driven 
inflation. We expect the Big Three to hike next in December as 
fuel prices are likely to stay elevated into early next year due to 
refining capacity constraints. Nevertheless, the Fed and ECB 
are likely to deliver less than around 60bps of rate hikes priced 
in by money markets over the next 12 months on the back of 
an eventual easing of oil and fuel prices leading to disinflation 
by H2 2027. This outlook means 3-7-year Developed Market 
investment grade bonds currently offer attractive risk-reward.  
Opportunity in Australian bonds. Other major central banks, 
such as the Bank of England, Bank of Canada and Reserve 
Bank of New Zealand are likely to hike rates by 25bps in the 
next two weeks given their focus on taming near-term inflation. 
However, we believe the Reserve Bank of Australia (RBA) is 
close to the end of its hiking cycle, even if it hikes one last time 
in the coming months (money markets give a 60% chance of a 
hike on 3 November). Australia’s domestic labour and housing 
markets and business confidence are softening after the RBA 
hiked its policy rate four times this year to a 15-year high. 
Meanwhile, Australia’s cash rate is 60bps higher than the US 
policy rate, supporting the AUD. We continue to see attractive 
income and FX gain opportunities from AUD corporate bonds.  
A ‘Blue Wave’ in the US? Prediction markets strongly favour 
Democrats taking the House of Representatives and possibly 
the Senate in the 3 November US mid-term elections. While an 
opposition sweep in the mid-terms has historically delivered the 
best 12-month returns for equities among all scenarios, partly 
due to the resulting policy gridlock, a decisive Democratic “blue 
wave” could increase longer-term risks of higher taxes and 
uncertainty over policy towards AI investment and data centres. 
With the Senate race still close and prediction markets fallible, 
we remain cautious on risk assets in the near term.  
Closing EU bank contingent convertible bonds (CoCos1) 
and US 10-year government bond ideas: We lock in 2% 
gains in EU CoCos and 1.7% gain in US 10-year government 
bond ideas amid French and US political risks (page 5-6). We 
continue to hedge any inflation risks through inflation-protected 
bonds and geopolitical risk through an Overweight in gold.  
Watching Big Tech earnings:  Upcoming Big Tech earnings 
raises uncertainty as market leadership remains concentrated 
and share-price reactions more mixed. With valuations less 
likely to drive returns, earnings delivery and sustainability of AI 
investment, funding and monetisation and broadening out of 
earnings beyond tech will be critical, raising the risk of volatility 
and sector divergence. We remain invested but defensively 
positioned, favouring diversification across sectors and regions.  
Staying defensive and diversified. We recently upgraded the 
defensive US Healthcare sector to Overweight, while retaining 
our Overweights to US tech and materials as well as financials 
in the US, Euro area and Japan. We also downgraded the more 
volatile Communications Services sector to Neutral and took 
profits on the semiconductor segment earlier this month before 
the latest correction. Our defensive and diversified stance 
should preserve participation in further equity upside, while 
reducing exposure to earnings disappointments and supporting 
resilience through the reporting season.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya
